<ide_selection>The user selected the lines 1 to 859 from /home/blu-bridge016/Downloads/Neural_Networks_exp_1926/BLUBLAS_kota/BluBridge-BLAS/src/level3/batched_gemm/BF16/SM103/Bgemm_sm103_bf16_TN_cluster.cu:
// Bgemm_sm103_bf16_TN_cluster.cu
// Blackwell Ultra (SM103a / B300) BF16 Batched GEMM — TN layout, 2-CTA cluster
// C[b,M,N] = alpha * A[b,K,M]^T × B[b,K,N] + beta * C[b,M,N]
//
// Architecture (mirrors kernel_v8 / ThunderKittens bf16_b200_gemm):
//   - tcgen05.mma.cta_group::2.kind::f16  (2-CTA cooperative MMA)
//   - CLC (Cluster Launch Control) persistent tile scheduling
//   - Hilbert-curve tile ordering for L2 cache locality
//   - 7 warps: 0-3 epilogue | 4 producer | 5 consumer | 6 scheduler
//   - 4D TMA bulk async A/B loads (batch dim), 2D TMA store for C
//   - Double-buffered TMEM (NUM_EPI_STAGES = 2)
//   - trans_a = 1, trans_b = 1 in i_desc for TN layout
//     A stored [K,M] → smem [BK × BM]
//     B stored [K,N] → smem [BK × BN/2] per CTA
//
// Each cluster: CTA0 → M cols [block_row0*BM..(block_row0+1)*BM), N cols
// [block_col*BN..+BN/2)
//               CTA1 → M cols [block_row1*BM..(block_row1+1)*BM), N cols
//               [block_col*BN+BN/2..+BN)

#include "mycublas.h"
#include <cstdint>
#include <cstdio>
#include <cstring>
#include <cuda.h>
#include <cuda_bf16.h>
#include <cuda_runtime.h>

// ─── compile-time constants
// ───────────────────────────────────────────────────

static constexpr int WARP_SIZE = 32;
static constexpr int SWIZZLE_W = 64;
static constexpr int MMA_K_STEP = 16;
static constexpr int CTA_GROUP_SIZE = 2;
static constexpr uint16_t CTA_MASK = 0b11;
// NUM_EPI_WARPS = BM/WARP_SIZE — computed inside the kernel template (scales
// with BM)
static constexpr int NUM_PROD_WARPS = 1;
static constexpr int NUM_CONS_WARPS = 1;
static constexpr int NUM_SCHED_WARPS = 1;
static constexpr int STORE_N = 64;
static constexpr int NUM_STORE_STGS = 2;
static constexpr int NUM_EPI_STAGES = 2;
static constexpr int NUM_CLC_STAGES = 2;

// ─── device primitives ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────

__device__ __forceinline__ uint32_t elect_sync() {
  uint32_t pred = 0;
  asm volatile("{\n\t"
               ".reg .pred %%px;\n\t"
               "elect.sync _|%%px, %1;\n\t"
               "@%%px mov.s32 %0, 1;\n\t"
               "}"
               : "+r"(pred)
               : "r"(0xFFFFFFFFu));
  return pred;
}

__device__ __forceinline__ void mbarrier_init(int addr, int count) {
  asm volatile("mbarrier.init.shared::cta.b64 [%0], %1;" ::"r"(addr),
               "r"(count));
}

__device__ __forceinline__ void mbarrier_arrive_expect(int addr, int tx) {
  asm volatile("mbarrier.arrive.expect_tx.release.cta.shared::cluster.b64 _, "
               "[%0], %1;" ::"r"(addr),
               "r"(tx)
               : "memory");
}

__device__ __forceinline__ void mbarrier_arrive_expect_cluster(int addr,
                                                               int tx) {
  asm volatile("mbarrier.arrive.expect_tx.release.cluster.shared::cluster.b64 "
               "_, [%0], %1;" ::"r"(addr),
               "r"(tx)
               : "memory");
}

__device__ __forceinline__ void mbarrier_arrive_cluster(int addr) {
  asm volatile(
      "mbarrier.arrive.release.cta.shared::cluster.b64 _, [%0];" ::"r"(addr)
      : "memory");
}

__device__ __forceinline__ void mbarrier_wait(int addr, int phase) {
  uint32_t ticks = 0x989680;
  asm volatile("{\n\t"
               ".reg .pred P;\n\t"
               "LAB_WAIT:\n\t"
               "mbarrier.try_wait.parity.acquire.cta.shared::cta.b64 P, [%0], "
               "%1, %2;\n\t"
               "@P bra.uni DONE;\n\t"
               "bra.uni LAB_WAIT;\n\t"
               "DONE:\n\t"
               "}" ::"r"(addr),
               "r"(phase), "r"(ticks));
}

__device__ __forceinline__ void cluster_fence_mbarrier_init() {
  asm volatile("fence.mbarrier_init.release.cluster;");
}

__device__ __forceinline__ void cluster_sync() {
  asm volatile("barrier.cluster.arrive.release.aligned;\n"
               "barrier.cluster.wait.acquire.aligned;\n" ::
                   : "memory");
}

__device__ __forceinline__ uint32_t get_cluster_cta_rank() {
  uint32_t r;
  asm volatile("mov.u32 %0, %%cluster_ctaid.x;" : "=r"(r));
  return r;
}

__device__ __forceinline__ uint32_t map_smem_addr_to_cta_rank(uint32_t addr,
                                                              uint32_t rank) {
  uint32_t r;
  asm volatile("mapa.shared::cluster.u32 %0, %1, %2;"
               : "=r"(r)
               : "r"(addr), "r"(rank));
  return r;
}

template <int CTA_GROUP = 1>
__device__ __forceinline__ void tma_4d_gmem2smem(int dst, const void *tmap,
                                                 int x, int y, int z, int w,
                                                 int mbar) {
  asm volatile("cp.async.bulk.tensor.4d.shared::cluster.global"
               ".mbarrier::complete_tx::bytes.cta_group::%7"
               " [%0], [%1, {%2, %3, %4, %5}], [%6];" ::"r"(dst),
               "l"(tmap), "r"(x), "r"(y), "r"(z), "r"(w), "r"(mbar),
               "n"(CTA_GROUP)
               : "memory");
}

__device__ __forceinline__ constexpr uint64_t desc_encode(uint64_t x) {
  return (x & 0x3'FFFFULL) >> 4ULL;
}

// PTX ISA 9.3 §9.7.17.4.1 (Table 45) — 64-bit smem descriptor layout:
//   bits  0-13 : matrix start address  (desc_encode)
//   bits 16-29 : Leading Dim Byte Offset (LBO)  — stride per m-block jump
//                in the leading dim (used when the operand spans > 1 m-block)
//   bits 32-45 : Stride Dim Byte Offset (SBO)   — stride per +8 in the
//                strided dim (always used)
//   bits 46-48 : fixed 0b001
//   bits 61-63 : swizzle mode (2 = 128-Byte swizzling)
__device__ __forceinline__ uint64_t make_smem_desc(int addr, int lbo_bytes,
                                                   int sbo_bytes) {
  return desc_encode(addr) | (desc_encode(lbo_bytes) << 16ULL) |
         (desc_encode(sbo_bytes) << 32ULL) | (1ULL << 46ULL) | (2ULL << 61ULL);
}

template <int CTA_GROUP = 1>
__device__ __forceinline__ void
tcgen05_mma_bf16(int tmem_addr, uint64_t a_desc, uint64_t b_desc,
                 uint32_t i_desc, int use_accum) {
  asm volatile("{\n\t"
               ".reg .pred p;\n\t"
               "setp.ne.b32 p, %4, 0;\n\t"
               "tcgen05.mma.cta_group::%5.kind::f16 [%0], %1, %2, %3, p;\n\t"
               "}" ::"r"(tmem_addr),
               "l"(a_desc), "l"(b_desc), "r"(i_desc), "r"(use_accum),
               "n"(CTA_GROUP));
}

template <int CTA_GROUP = 1>
__device__ __forceinline__ void alloc_tmem(int tmem_smem_addr, int width) {
  asm volatile(
      "tcgen05.alloc.cta_group::%2.sync.aligned.shared::cta.b32 [%0], %1;" ::
          "r"(tmem_smem_addr),
      "r"(width), "n"(CTA_GROUP));
}

template <int CTA_GROUP = 1>
__device__ __forceinline__ void dealloc_tmem(int tmem_addr, int width) {
  asm volatile(
      "tcgen05.dealloc.cta_group::%2.sync.aligned.b32 %0, %1;" ::"r"(tmem_addr),
      "r"(width), "n"(CTA_GROUP));
}

template <int CTA_GROUP = 2>
__device__ __forceinline__ void tcgen05_commit_multicast(int mbar_addr,
                                                         uint16_t mask) {
  asm volatile("tcgen05.commit.cta_group::%2"
               ".mbarrier::arrive::one.shared::cluster.multicast::cluster.b64 "
               "[%0], %1;" ::"r"(mbar_addr),
               "h"(mask), "n"(CTA_GROUP)
               : "memory");
}

__device__ __forceinline__ void tcgen05_ld(float *tmp, int addr) {
  asm volatile(
      "tcgen05.ld.sync.aligned.32x32b.x8.b32 {%0,%1,%2,%3,%4,%5,%6,%7}, [%8];"
      : "=f"(tmp[0]), "=f"(tmp[1]), "=f"(tmp[2]), "=f"(tmp[3]), "=f"(tmp[4]),
        "=f"(tmp[5]), "=f"(tmp[6]), "=f"(tmp[7])
      : "r"(addr));
}

__device__ __forceinline__ void tcgen05_wait_ld() {
  asm volatile("tcgen05.wait::ld.sync.aligned;");
}
__device__ __forceinline__ void tcgen05_fence_before() {
  asm volatile("tcgen05.fence::before_thread_sync;");
}
__device__ __forceinline__ void tcgen05_fence_after() {
  asm volatile("tcgen05.fence::after_thread_sync;");
}
__device__ __forceinline__ void tma_store_fence() {
  asm volatile("fence.proxy.async.shared::cta;" ::: "memory");
}
__device__ __forceinline__ void tma_store_commit() {
  asm volatile("cp.async.bulk.commit_group;" ::: "memory");
}

template <int N> __device__ __forceinline__ void tma_store_wait() {
  asm volatile("cp.async.bulk.wait_group %0;" ::"n"(N) : "memory");
}

__device__ __forceinline__ void tma_2d_smem2gmem(int src, const void *tmap,
                                                 int x, int y) {
  asm volatile("cp.async.bulk.tensor.2d.global.shared::cta.bulk_group [%0, "
               "{%2, %3}], [%1];" ::"l"(tmap),
               "r"(src), "r"(x), "r"(y)
               : "memory");
}

__device__ __forceinline__ void barrier_sync(int bar, int n) {
  asm volatile("bar.sync %0, %1;" ::"r"(bar), "r"(n));
}

// ─── CLC primitives ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

__device__ __forceinline__ void clc_try_cancel(int response_addr,
                                               int mbar_addr) {
  asm volatile("clusterlaunchcontrol.try_cancel.async.shared::cta"
               ".mbarrier::complete_tx::bytes.multicast::cluster::all.b128 "
               "[%0], [%1];" ::"r"(response_addr),
               "r"(mbar_addr)
               : "memory");
}

__device__ __forceinline__ void
clc_query_response(int response_addr, uint32_t &is_valid, uint32_t &new_ctaid) {
  asm volatile(
      "{\n\t"
      ".reg .b128 handle;\n\t"
      ".reg .pred p;\n\t"
      "mov.s32 %0, 0;\n\t"
      "ld.shared.b128 handle, [%2];\n\t"
      "clusterlaunchcontrol.query_cancel.is_canceled.pred.b128 p, handle;\n\t"
      "@p mov.s32 %0, 1;\n\t"
      "clusterlaunchcontrol.query_cancel.get_first_ctaid::x.b32.b128 %1, "
      "handle;\n\t"
      "}"
      : "=r"(is_valid), "=r"(new_ctaid)
      : "r"(response_addr)
      : "memory");
}

// ─── host-side helpers
// ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

static inline void check_cu(CUresult e) {
  if (e != CUDA_SUCCESS) {
    const char *msg = nullptr;
    cuGetErrorString(e, &msg);
    fprintf(stderr, "[BluBridge TMA] %s (%d)\n", msg ? msg : "unknown", (int)e);
    exit(EXIT_FAILURE);
  }
}

// 4D TMA for A TN: A[batch, K, M] row-major, box = [64, BK, BM/64, 1]
// Each CTA loads its BM M-columns (step z by block_row * BM / 64)
static inline void init_4d_tma_A_TN(CUtensorMap *tmap, const __nv_bfloat16 *ptr,
                                    int M, int K, int BM, int BK,
                                    uint64_t batchCount, uint64_t batchStride,
                                    CUtensorMapSwizzle sw) {
  constexpr uint32_t rank = 4;
  uint64_t globalDim[rank] = {64ULL, (uint64_t)K, (uint64_t)(M / 64),
                              batchCount};
  uint64_t globalStrides[rank - 1] = {(uint64_t)M * sizeof(__nv_bfloat16),
                                      128ULL,
                                      batchStride * sizeof(__nv_bfloat16)};
  uint32_t boxDim[rank] = {64U, (uint32_t)BK, (uint32_t)(BM / 64), 1U};
  uint32_t elementStrides[rank] = {1U, 1U, 1U, 1U};
  check_cu(cuTensorMapEncodeTiled(
      tmap, CU_TENSOR_MAP_DATA_TYPE_BFLOAT16, rank, (void *)ptr, globalDim,
      globalStrides, boxDim, elementStrides, CU_TENSOR_MAP_INTERLEAVE_NONE, sw,
      CU_TENSOR_MAP_L2_PROMOTION_NONE, CU_TENSOR_MAP_FLOAT_OOB_FILL_NONE));
}

// 4D TMA for B TN: B[batch, K, N] row-major, box = [64, BK, BN/(2*64), 1]
static inline void init_4d_tma_B_TN(CUtensorMap *tmap, const __nv_bfloat16 *ptr,
                                    int N, int K, int BN, int BK,
                                    uint64_t batchCount, uint64_t batchStride,
                                    CUtensorMapSwizzle sw) {
  constexpr uint32_t rank = 4;
  uint64_t globalDim[rank] = {64ULL, (uint64_t)K, (uint64_t)(N / 64),
                              batchCount};
  uint64_t globalStrides[rank - 1] = {(uint64_t)N * sizeof(__nv_bfloat16),
                                      128ULL,
                                      batchStride * sizeof(__nv_bfloat16)};
  uint32_t boxDim[rank] = {64U, (uint32_t)BK,
                           (uint32_t)(BN / CTA_GROUP_SIZE / 64), 1U};
  uint32_t elementStrides[rank] = {1U, 1U, 1U, 1U};
  check_cu(cuTensorMapEncodeTiled(
      tmap, CU_TENSOR_MAP_DATA_TYPE_BFLOAT16, rank, (void *)ptr, globalDim,
      globalStrides, boxDim, elementStrides, CU_TENSOR_MAP_INTERLEAVE_NONE, sw,
      CU_TENSOR_MAP_L2_PROMOTION_NONE, CU_TENSOR_MAP_FLOAT_OOB_FILL_NONE));
}

// 2D TMA for C store: flattened [batch*M, N], box = [STORE_N, BM]
static inline void init_2d_tma_C(CUtensorMap *tmap, const __nv_bfloat16 *ptr,
                                 uint64_t totalRows, uint64_t totalCols,
                                 uint32_t boxRows, uint32_t boxCols,
                                 CUtensorMapSwizzle sw) {
  constexpr uint32_t rank = 2;
  uint64_t globalDim[rank] = {totalCols, totalRows};
  uint64_t globalStrides[rank - 1] = {totalCols * sizeof(__nv_bfloat16)};
  uint32_t boxDim[rank] = {boxCols, boxRows};
  uint32_t elementStrides[rank] = {1U, 1U};
  check_cu(cuTensorMapEncodeTiled(
      tmap, CU_TENSOR_MAP_DATA_TYPE_BFLOAT16, rank, (void *)ptr, globalDim,
      globalStrides, boxDim, elementStrides, CU_TENSOR_MAP_INTERLEAVE_NONE, sw,
      CU_TENSOR_MAP_L2_PROMOTION_NONE, CU_TENSOR_MAP_FLOAT_OOB_FILL_NONE));
}

// ─── kernel
// ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

template <int BM, int BN, int BK, int QUEUE_SIZE>
__global__ __cluster_dims__(2, 1, 1) void bgemm_sm103_bf16_tn_cluster_kernel(
    const __nv_bfloat16 *__restrict__ C_in, __nv_bfloat16 *__restrict__ C,
    int M, int N, int K, int total_mn_tiles, float alpha, float beta,
    const __grid_constant__ CUtensorMap A_tmap,
    const __grid_constant__ CUtensorMap B_tmap,
    const __grid_constant__ CUtensorMap C_tmap, int grid_n) {
  const int tid = threadIdx.x;
  const int warp_id = tid / WARP_SIZE;
  const int lane_id = tid % WARP_SIZE;
  const uint32_t cta_rank = get_cluster_cta_rank();

  extern __shared__ __align__(1024) char smem[];
  const int smem_ptr = static_cast<int>(__cvta_generic_to_shared(smem));

#pragma nv_diag_suppress static_var_with_dynamic_init
  __shared__ int tmem[1];
  const int tmem_smem_addr = static_cast<int>(__cvta_generic_to_shared(tmem));

  __shared__ __align__(8) uint64_t full_mbar[QUEUE_SIZE];
  __shared__ __align__(8) uint64_t empty_mbar[QUEUE_SIZE];
  __shared__ __align__(8) uint64_t tmem_full[NUM_EPI_STAGES];
  __shared__ __align__(8) uint64_t tmem_empty[NUM_EPI_STAGES];
  __shared__ __align__(16) uint8_t clc_response[NUM_CLC_STAGES][16];
  __shared__ __align__(8) uint64_t clc_full_mbar[NUM_CLC_STAGES];
  __shared__ __align__(8) uint64_t clc_empty_mbar[NUM_CLC_STAGES];

  const int full_mbar_addr =
      static_cast<int>(__cvta_generic_to_shared(full_mbar));
  const int empty_mbar_addr =
      static_cast<int>(__cvta_generic_to_shared(empty_mbar));
  const int tmem_full_addr =
      static_cast<int>(__cvta_generic_to_shared(tmem_full));
  const int tmem_empty_addr =
      static_cast<int>(__cvta_generic_to_shared(tmem_empty));
  const int clc_response_addr =
      static_cast<int>(__cvta_generic_to_shared(clc_response));
  const int clc_full_mbar_addr =
      static_cast<int>(__cvta_generic_to_shared(clc_full_mbar));
  const int clc_empty_mbar_addr =
      static_cast<int>(__cvta_generic_to_shared(clc_empty_mbar));

  constexpr int NUM_EPI_WARPS = BM / WARP_SIZE;
  constexpr int CLC_EMPTY_ARRIVE =
      (NUM_EPI_WARPS + NUM_PROD_WARPS + NUM_CONS_WARPS + NUM_SCHED_WARPS) *
      CTA_GROUP_SIZE;
  constexpr int producer_warp = NUM_EPI_WARPS;
  constexpr int consumer_warp = NUM_EPI_WARPS + 1;
  constexpr int scheduler_warp = NUM_EPI_WARPS + 2;

  // A smem: [BK × BM] per CTA (trans_a=1: A stored [K,M])
  // B smem: [BK × BN/2] per CTA (trans_b=1: B stored [K,N])
  constexpr int a_tile_bytes = BK * BM * (int)sizeof(__nv_bfloat16);
  constexpr int b_tile_bytes =
      BK * (BN / CTA_GROUP_SIZE) * (int)sizeof(__nv_bfloat16);
  constexpr int copy_size = a_tile_bytes + b_tile_bytes;
  constexpr int store_buf_sz = BM * STORE_N * (int)sizeof(__nv_bfloat16);
  const int store_smem = smem_ptr + QUEUE_SIZE * copy_size;
  __nv_bfloat16 *store_base =
      reinterpret_cast<__nv_bfloat16 *>(smem + QUEUE_SIZE * copy_size);

  if (warp_id == producer_warp && elect_sync()) {
    for (int i = 0; i < QUEUE_SIZE; ++i) {
      mbarrier_init(full_mbar_addr + i * 8, NUM_PROD_WARPS * CTA_GROUP_SIZE);
      mbarrier_init(empty_mbar_addr + i * 8, 1);
    }
    for (int i = 0; i < NUM_EPI_STAGES; ++i) {
      mbarrier_init(tmem_full_addr + i * 8, 1);
      mbarrier_init(tmem_empty_addr + i * 8, NUM_EPI_WARPS * CTA_GROUP_SIZE);
    }
    for (int i = 0; i < NUM_CLC_STAGES; ++i) {
      mbarrier_init(clc_full_mbar_addr + i * 8, 1);
      mbarrier_init(clc_empty_mbar_addr + i * 8, CLC_EMPTY_ARRIVE);
    }
    cluster_fence_mbarrier_init();
  } else if (warp_id == consumer_warp) {
    alloc_tmem<CTA_GROUP_SIZE>(tmem_smem_addr, BN * NUM_EPI_STAGES);
  }

  cluster_sync();

  const int tmem_addr = tmem[0];
  const int k_iters = K / BK;

  // TN: trans_a=1 (bit 15), trans_b=1 (bit 16), M = CTA_GROUP_SIZE * BM
  constexpr uint32_t i_desc = (1U << 4U) | (1U << 7U) | (1U << 10U) |
                              (1U << 15U)   // trans_a = 1
                              | (1U << 16U) // trans_b = 1
                              | ((uint32_t)(BN) >> 3U << 17U) |
                              ((uint32_t)(CTA_GROUP_SIZE * BM) >> 4U << 24U);

  const int tma_mbar_base = (cta_rank == 0)
                                ? full_mbar_addr
                                : map_smem_addr_to_cta_rank(full_mbar_addr, 0);

  const int cta0_clc_empty_addr =
      (cta_rank == 0) ? clc_empty_mbar_addr
                      : map_smem_addr_to_cta_rank(clc_empty_mbar_addr, 0);

  const int init_tile_idx = (int)(blockIdx.x / CTA_GROUP_SIZE);
  int batch = init_tile_idx / total_mn_tiles;
  int mn_tile = init_tile_idx % total_mn_tiles;

  // ── producer warp ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  if (warp_id == producer_warp && elect_sync()) {
    int block_col = mn_tile % grid_n;
    int block_row = (mn_tile / grid_n) * CTA_GROUP_SIZE + (int)cta_rank;
    int cur_batch = batch;

    int stage = 0, phase = 0, issued = 0;
    int clc_stage = 0, clc_full_phase = 0;

    while (true) {
      // A[K,M]: select M-columns for this CTA (z = block_row * BM / 64)
      const int A_col = block_row * BM / SWIZZLE_W;
      // B[K,N]: select N-columns for this CTA
      const int B_col =
          (block_col * BN + (int)cta_rank * (BN / CTA_GROUP_SIZE)) / SWIZZLE_W;

      for (int k = 0; k < k_iters; ++k) {
        if (issued >= QUEUE_SIZE)
          mbarrier_wait(empty_mbar_addr + stage * 8, phase ^ 1);
        ++issued;

        const int A_smem = smem_ptr + stage * copy_size;
        const int B_smem = A_smem + a_tile_bytes;
        const int mbar = tma_mbar_base + stage * 8;

        mbarrier_arrive_expect(mbar, copy_size);

        // A TN: y=k*BK (K row), z=A_col (M column group)
        tma_4d_gmem2smem<CTA_GROUP_SIZE>(A_smem, &A_tmap, 0, k * BK, A_col,
                                         cur_batch, mbar);
        // B TN: y=k*BK (K row), z=B_col (N column group)
        tma_4d_gmem2smem<CTA_GROUP_SIZE>(B_smem, &B_tmap, 0, k * BK, B_col,
                                         cur_batch, mbar);

        stage = (stage + 1) % QUEUE_SIZE;
        phase ^= (stage == 0);
      }

      mbarrier_wait(clc_full_mbar_addr + clc_stage * 8, clc_full_phase);
      uint32_t is_valid, new_ctaid;
      clc_query_response(clc_response_addr + clc_stage * 16, is_valid,
                         new_ctaid);
      mbarrier_arrive_cluster(cta0_clc_empty_addr + clc_stage * 8);
      clc_stage = (clc_stage + 1) % NUM_CLC_STAGES;
      if (clc_stage == 0)
        clc_full_phase ^= 1;

      if (!is_valid)
        break;

      const int new_tile_idx = (int)(new_ctaid / CTA_GROUP_SIZE);
      cur_batch = new_tile_idx / total_mn_tiles;
      block_col = (new_tile_idx % total_mn_tiles) % grid_n;
      block_row = ((new_tile_idx % total_mn_tiles) / grid_n) * CTA_GROUP_SIZE +
                  (int)cta_rank;
      issued = 0;
    }
  }
  // ── consumer warp ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  else if (warp_id == consumer_warp && elect_sync()) {
    int stage = 0, phase = 0;
    int wave_iter = 0;
    int clc_stage = 0, clc_full_phase = 0;

    while (true) {
      if (cta_rank == 0) {
        const int wave_stage = wave_iter % NUM_EPI_STAGES;
        const int wave_phase = (wave_iter / NUM_EPI_STAGES) & 1;

        if (wave_iter >= NUM_EPI_STAGES)
          mbarrier_wait(tmem_empty_addr + wave_stage * 8, wave_phase ^ 1);

        tcgen05_fence_after();

        for (int k = 0; k < k_iters; ++k) {
          mbarrier_wait(full_mbar_addr + stage * 8, phase);
          tcgen05_fence_after();

          const int A_smem = smem_ptr + stage * copy_size;
          const int B_smem = A_smem + a_tile_bytes;

          for (int k1 = 0; k1 < BK / SWIZZLE_W; ++k1) {
            for (int k2 = 0; k2 < SWIZZLE_W / MMA_K_STEP; ++k2) {
              const int use_accum = (k == 0 && k1 == 0 && k2 == 0) ? 0 : 1;

              // TN smem layout after TMA + 128B swizzle is CHUNK-MAJOR (NOT raw
              // row-major):
              //   A smem = [BM/64 chunks][BK rows][64-element swizzled atoms ×
              //   128 B each] B smem = [BN/CTA/64 chunks][BK rows][64-element
              //   atoms × 128 B each]
              // Within a chunk: +1 K-row = +128 B (atom row width), not +BM*2
              // B. Reference: ThunderKittens descriptor.cuh + DeepGEMM
              // mma/sm100.cuh (both agree).
              //
              // a_off / b_off advance through K dimension within a CHUNK:
              //   per +16 K (one MMA_K_STEP) = +16 * SWIZZLE_W * sizeof(bf16) =
              //   +2048 B
              // (NOT +16 * BM * sizeof(bf16) — that's the raw-row-major
              // mistake)
              const int a_off =
                  k1 * SWIZZLE_W * SWIZZLE_W * (int)sizeof(__nv_bfloat16) +
                  k2 * MMA_K_STEP * SWIZZLE_W * (int)sizeof(__nv_bfloat16);

              const int b_off =
                  k1 * SWIZZLE_W * SWIZZLE_W * (int)sizeof(__nv_bfloat16) +
                  k2 * MMA_K_STEP * SWIZZLE_W * (int)sizeof(__nv_bfloat16);

              // make_smem_desc LBO/SBO for MN-major 128B swizzle bf16:
              //   LBO = BK * SWIZZLE_W * sizeof(bf16) = 8192 B for BK=64
              //         (byte stride to next M-chunk of 64 elements; needed
              //         because BM>64)
              //   SBO = 8 * SWIZZLE_W * sizeof(bf16) = 1024 B  (constant)
              //         (byte stride per +8 K-rows within a chunk)
              tcgen05_mma_bf16<CTA_GROUP_SIZE>(
                  tmem_addr + wave_stage * BN,
                  make_smem_desc(A_smem + a_off,
                                 BK * SWIZZLE_W * (int)sizeof(__nv_bfloat16),
                                 8 * SWIZZLE_W * (int)sizeof(__nv_bfloat16)),
                  make_smem_desc(B_smem + b_off,
                                 BK * SWIZZLE_W * (int)sizeof(__nv_bfloat16),
                                 8 * SWIZZLE_W * (int)sizeof(__nv_bfloat16)),
                  i_desc, use_accum);
            }
          }

          tcgen05_commit_multicast<CTA_GROUP_SIZE>(empty_mbar_addr + stage * 8,
                                                   CTA_MASK);
          stage = (stage + 1) % QUEUE_SIZE;
          phase ^= (stage == 0);
        }

        tcgen05_commit_multicast<CTA_GROUP_SIZE>(
            tmem_full_addr + wave_stage * 8, CTA_MASK);
        wave_iter++;
      }

      mbarrier_wait(clc_full_mbar_addr + clc_stage * 8, clc_full_phase);
      uint32_t is_valid, new_ctaid;
      clc_query_response(clc_response_addr + clc_stage * 16, is_valid,
                         new_ctaid);
      mbarrier_arrive_cluster(cta0_clc_empty_addr + clc_stage * 8);
      clc_stage = (clc_stage + 1) % NUM_CLC_STAGES;
      if (clc_stage == 0)
        clc_full_phase ^= 1;

      if (!is_valid)
        break;
    }
  }
  // ── scheduler warp ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  else if (warp_id == scheduler_warp && elect_sync()) {
    if (cta_rank == 0) {
#pragma unroll
      for (int s = 0; s < NUM_CLC_STAGES; ++s) {
        mbarrier_arrive_expect_cluster(clc_full_mbar_addr + s * 8, 16);
        int remote_full =
            map_smem_addr_to_cta_rank(clc_full_mbar_addr + s * 8, 1);
        mbarrier_arrive_expect_cluster(remote_full, 16);
        clc_try_cancel(clc_response_addr + s * 16, clc_full_mbar_addr + s * 8);
      }
    }

    int clc_stage = 0, clc_full_phase = 0, clc_empty_phase = 0;

    while (true) {
      mbarrier_wait(clc_full_mbar_addr + clc_stage * 8, clc_full_phase);
      uint32_t is_valid, new_ctaid;
      clc_query_response(clc_response_addr + clc_stage * 16, is_valid,
                         new_ctaid);
      mbarrier_arrive_cluster(cta0_clc_empty_addr + clc_stage * 8);

      int issue_stage = clc_stage;
      clc_stage = (clc_stage + 1) % NUM_CLC_STAGES;
      if (clc_stage == 0)
        clc_full_phase ^= 1;

      if (!is_valid)
        break;

      if (cta_rank == 0) {
        mbarrier_wait(clc_empty_mbar_addr + issue_stage * 8, clc_empty_phase);
        if (issue_stage == NUM_CLC_STAGES - 1)
          clc_empty_phase ^= 1;

        mbarrier_arrive_expect_cluster(clc_full_mbar_addr + issue_stage * 8,
                                       16);
        int remote_full =
            map_smem_addr_to_cta_rank(clc_full_mbar_addr + issue_stage * 8, 1);
        mbarrier_arrive_expect_cluster(remote_full, 16);
        clc_try_cancel(clc_response_addr + issue_stage * 16,
                       clc_full_mbar_addr + issue_stage * 8);
      }
    }
  }
  // ── epilogue warps ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────
  else if (warp_id < NUM_EPI_WARPS) {
    constexpr int EPI_BAR = 7;
    constexpr int EPI_THREADS = NUM_EPI_WARPS * WARP_SIZE;
    constexpr int num_chunks = BN / STORE_N;
    constexpr int LOADS_PER = STORE_N / 8;

    int block_col = mn_tile % grid_n;
    int block_row = (mn_tile / grid_n) * CTA_GROUP_SIZE + (int)cta_rank;
    int cur_batch = batch;

    int wave_iter = 0;
    int clc_stage = 0, clc_full_phase = 0;

    while (true) {
      const int wave_stage = wave_iter % NUM_EPI_STAGES;
      const int wave_phase = (wave_iter / NUM_EPI_STAGES) & 1;

      mbarrier_wait(tmem_full_addr + wave_stage * 8, wave_phase);
      tcgen05_fence_after();

      // CTA0 → rows [0..BM), CTA1 → rows [BM..2*BM) in TMEM
      const int tmem_row = (tmem_addr + wave_stage * BN) +
                           ((int(cta_rank) * BM + warp_id * WARP_SIZE) << 16);
      const int row = warp_id * WARP_SIZE + lane_id;
      int store_stage = 0;

      for (int chunk = 0; chunk < num_chunks; ++chunk) {
        if (warp_id == 0)
          tma_store_wait<NUM_STORE_STGS - 1>();

        float tmp[LOADS_PER][8];
#pragma unroll
        for (int n = 0; n < LOADS_PER; ++n)
          tcgen05_ld(tmp[n], tmem_row + chunk * STORE_N + n * 8);
        tcgen05_wait_ld();

        if (chunk == num_chunks - 1) {
          tcgen05_fence_before();
          const int tmem_empty_cta0 =
              (tmem_empty_addr + wave_stage * 8) & 0xFEFFFFFF;
          if (elect_sync())
            mbarrier_arrive_cluster(tmem_empty_cta0);
        }

        barrier_sync(EPI_BAR, EPI_THREADS);

#pragma unroll
        for (int n = 0; n < LOADS_PER; ++n) {
          nv_bfloat162 packed_bf[4];
#pragma unroll
          for (int i = 0; i < 4; ++i) {
            float v0 = tmp[n][i * 2] * alpha;
            float v1 = tmp[n][i * 2 + 1] * alpha;
            packed_bf[i] = __float22bfloat162_rn({v0, v1});
          }

          if (beta != 0.0f) {
            const int c_col = block_col * BN + chunk * STORE_N + n * 8;
            const int c_row = cur_batch * M + block_row * BM + row;
            const __nv_bfloat16 *cp = C_in + (long long)c_row * N + c_col;
#pragma unroll
            for (int i = 0; i < 4; ++i) {
              __nv_bfloat162 old =
                  *reinterpret_cast<const __nv_bfloat162 *>(cp + i * 2);
              packed_bf[i] =
                  __hadd2(packed_bf[i],
                          __hmul2(old, __float22bfloat162_rn({beta, beta})));
            }
          }

          const int swizzled_n = n ^ (row & 7);
          __nv_bfloat16 *wp = store_base + store_stage * BM * STORE_N +
                              row * STORE_N + swizzled_n * 8;
          *reinterpret_cast<int4 *>(wp) = *reinterpret_cast<int4 *>(packed_bf);
        }

        __syncwarp();
        tma_store_fence();
        barrier_sync(EPI_BAR, EPI_THREADS);

        if (warp_id == 0 && elect_sync()) {
          const int src = store_smem + store_stage * store_buf_sz;
          const int c_col = block_col * BN + chunk * STORE_N;
          const int c_row = cur_batch * M + block_row * BM;
          tma_2d_smem2gmem(src, &C_tmap, c_col, c_row);
          tma_store_commit();
        }
        store_stage ^= 1;
      }

      wave_iter++;

      mbarrier_wait(clc_full_mbar_addr + clc_stage * 8, clc_full_phase);
      uint32_t is_valid, new_ctaid;
      clc_query_response(clc_response_addr + clc_stage * 16, is_valid,
                         new_ctaid);
      if (elect_sync())
        mbarrier_arrive_cluster(cta0_clc_empty_addr + clc_stage * 8);
      clc_stage = (clc_stage + 1) % NUM_CLC_STAGES;
      if (clc_stage == 0)
        clc_full_phase ^= 1;

      if (!is_valid)
        break;

      const int new_tile_idx = (int)(new_ctaid / CTA_GROUP_SIZE);
      cur_batch = new_tile_idx / total_mn_tiles;
      block_col = (new_tile_idx % total_mn_tiles) % grid_n;
      block_row = ((new_tile_idx % total_mn_tiles) / grid_n) * CTA_GROUP_SIZE +
                  (int)cta_rank;
    }

    if (warp_id == 0)
      tma_store_wait<0>();
    barrier_sync(EPI_BAR, EPI_THREADS);
  }

  cluster_sync();
  if (warp_id == 0)
    dealloc_tmem<CTA_GROUP_SIZE>(tmem_addr, BN * NUM_EPI_STAGES);
}

// ─── launcher
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

template <int BM, int BN, int BK>
static void
launch_bgemm_tn_cluster(const __nv_bfloat16 *A, const __nv_bfloat16 *B,
                        __nv_bfloat16 *C, int M, int N, int K, int batchCount,
                        float alpha, float beta, cudaStream_t stream) {
  static int s_arch_ok = -1;
  if (s_arch_ok < 0) {
    int dev;
    cudaGetDevice(&dev);
    cudaDeviceProp prop;
    cudaGetDeviceProperties(&prop, dev);
    s_arch_ok = (prop.major == 10 && prop.minor >= 3) ? 1 : 0;
    if (!s_arch_ok)
      fprintf(stderr,
              "[BluBridge SM103 TN Cluster] WARNING: device '%s' SM %d.%d, "
              "requires SM 10.3+\n",
              prop.name, prop.major, prop.minor);
  }
  if (!s_arch_ok)
    return;

  // A smem [BK × BM], B smem [BK × BN/2]
  constexpr int a_tile = BK * BM * (int)sizeof(__nv_bfloat16);
  constexpr int b_tile =
      BK * (BN / CTA_GROUP_SIZE) * (int)sizeof(__nv_bfloat16);
  constexpr int tile_size = a_tile + b_tile;
  constexpr int store_tot =
      NUM_STORE_STGS * BM * STORE_N * (int)sizeof(__nv_bfloat16);
  constexpr int smem_budget = 227 * 1024;
  constexpr int QUEUE_SIZE = (smem_budget - store_tot) / tile_size;
  static_assert(QUEUE_SIZE >= 2, "QUEUE_SIZE too small");
  constexpr int smem_size = QUEUE_SIZE * tile_size + store_tot;

  CUtensorMap A_tmap, B_tmap, C_tmap;
  init_4d_tma_A_TN(&A_tmap, A, M, K, BM, BK, (uint64_t)batchCount,
                   (uint64_t)K * M, CU_TENSOR_MAP_SWIZZLE_128B);
  init_4d_tma_B_TN(&B_tmap, B, N, K, BN, BK, (uint64_t)batchCount,
                   (uint64_t)K * N, CU_TENSOR_MAP_SWIZZLE_128B);
  init_2d_tma_C(&C_tmap, C, (uint64_t)batchCount * M, (uint64_t)N, (uint32_t)BM,
                (uint32_t)STORE_N, CU_TENSOR_MAP_SWIZZLE_128B);

  // Ceil-div so shapes smaller than one tile still get grid >= 1.
  // TMA's OOB-fill handles partial tiles cleanly; 2D TMA store clips writes
  // to the actual matrix bounds. Without ceil-div, M=128 (smaller than the
  // 2*BM=256 cluster tile) gives grid_m=0 → CUDA rejects launch with
  // "invalid argument".
  const int grid_m = (M + CTA_GROUP_SIZE * BM - 1) / (CTA_GROUP_SIZE * BM);
  const int grid_n = (N + BN - 1) / BN;
  const int total_mn_tiles = grid_m * grid_n;

  auto kernel = bgemm_sm103_bf16_tn_cluster_kernel<BM, BN, BK, QUEUE_SIZE>;
  cudaFuncSetAttribute(kernel, cudaFuncAttributeMaxDynamicSharedMemorySize,
                       smem_size);

  dim3 grid(total_mn_tiles * batchCount * CTA_GROUP_SIZE);
  const int block_size =
      (BM / WARP_SIZE + NUM_PROD_WARPS + NUM_CONS_WARPS + NUM_SCHED_WARPS) *
      WARP_SIZE;
  kernel<<<grid, block_size, smem_size, stream>>>(C, C, M, N, K, total_mn_tiles,
                                                  alpha, beta, A_tmap, B_tmap,
                                                  C_tmap, grid_n);

  cudaError_t err = cudaGetLastError();
  if (err != cudaSuccess)
    fprintf(stderr, "[BluBridge SM103 TN Cluster] Launch error: %s\n",
            cudaGetErrorString(err));
}

// ─── multi-config dispatcher (ThunderKittens-style) ──────────────────────────

static void dispatch_bgemm_tn_cluster(const __nv_bfloat16 *A,
                                      const __nv_bfloat16 *B, __nv_bfloat16 *C,
                                      int M, int N, int K, int batchCount,
                                      float alpha, float beta,
                                      cudaStream_t stream) {
  launch_bgemm_tn_cluster<128, 128, 64>(A, B, C, M, N, K, batchCount, alpha,
                                        beta, stream);
}

// ─── public C API
// ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────

extern "C" void mycublasBgemmSM103_bf16_tn_cluster_128x128x64(
    mycublasHandle_t handle, int M, int N, int K, float alpha,
    const __nv_bfloat16 *A, const __nv_bfloat16 *B, float beta,
    __nv_bfloat16 *C, int batchCount) {
  cudaStream_t stream = handle ? handle->stream : 0;
  dispatch_bgemm_tn_cluster(A, B, C, M, N, K, batchCount, alpha, beta, stream);
}

extern "C" void mycublasBgemmSM103_bf16_tn_cluster_256x256x64(
    mycublasHandle_t handle, int M, int N, int K, float alpha,
    const __nv_bfloat16 *A, const __nv_bfloat16 *B, float beta,
    __nv_bfloat16 *C, int batchCount) {
  cudaStream_t stream = handle ? handle->stream : 0;
  if (M % (CTA_GROUP_SIZE * 256) == 0)
    launch_bgemm_tn_cluster<256, 256, 64>(A, B, C, M, N, K, batchCount, alpha,
                                          beta, stream);
  else
    dispatch_bgemm_tn_cluster(A, B, C, M, N, K, batchCount, alpha, beta,
                              stream);
}

### CLAUDE [2026-06-30 18:34 IST]

# ThunderKittens uses **FLOOR DIV** — your colleague uses **CEIL DIV** (more permissive)

## What TK actually does

[`ThunderKittens/kernels/gemm/bf16_b200/bf16_b200_gemm.cu:50`](ThunderKittens/kernels/gemm/bf16_b200/bf16_b200_gemm.cu#L50):

```cpp
__host__ __inline__ dim3 grid() {
    return dim3( d.rows() / (C::NUM_CONSUMERS*C::Mb/2) * d.cols() / C::Nb );
    //                ↑ FLOOR div                       ↑ FLOOR div
}
```

And line 74-75:
```cpp
const int rblks = g.d.rows() / (C::Mb * C::NUM_CONSUMERS);   // FLOOR
const int cblks = g.d.cols() / C::Nb;                         // FLOOR
```

**TK is floor-div everywhere.** Same as our original (broken-for-tiny) code.

## Three-way comparison

| | Grid math | Behavior for shape NOT multiple of tile |
|---|---|---|
| **ThunderKittens** | floor div | grid = 0 → would also fail launch (TK probably assumes user pre-aligns) |
| **Our original** | floor div | grid = 0 → "invalid argument" launch error (what you saw) |
| **Our colleague** | **ceil div** | grid = 1 → kernel launches; TMA OOB-fill handles the partial tile cleanly |
| **Our new fix (after my edit)** | **ceil div** | same as colleague ✓ |

## So why does TK get away with floor div?

Look at TK's test/demo shapes — they're always multiples of the tile size (e.g., `Mb=256`, test M values like 1024, 2048, 4096). TK essentially says: **"the caller's job to ensure M, N are tile-aligned"**. If you give TK a misaligned shape, it would fail to launch (or compute the wrong thing), just like our floor-div code did on `tiny`.

Your colleague made the kernel **more user-friendly** than TK by using ceil-div + relying on TMA's OOB-fill behavior for partial tiles. This is a **legitimate choice** that production GEMM libraries (cuBLAS, CUTLASS) also make for general-purpose usage.

## Is this safe? Yes — and here's exactly why

Three things together guarantee correctness for misaligned shapes:

| Component | What it does for OOB regions |
|---|---|
| **TMA load (A, B)** | `CU_TENSOR_MAP_FLOAT_OOB_FILL_NONE` → reads zero for out-of-bounds positions |
| **MMA computation** | computes against the OOB-zero data → produces zero contribution to the output |
| **TMA store (C)** | bounds-checks against `globalDim={N, M}` → clips writes that fall outside the matrix |

So for `tiny (M=128)` with `BM=128`, `CTA_GROUP=2`:
1. Cluster launches with grid_m=1 (covers M=0..255)
2. CTA 0 reads A[K, 0..127] correctly, computes C[0..127, :] correctly
3. CTA 1 attempts to read A[K, 128..255] → TMA fills zeros → CTA 1 computes zeros
4. CTA 0 stores C[0..127, :] → valid, written to gmem
5. CTA 1 stores C[128..255, :] → TMA clips: M=128..255 is outside `globalDim` → store is silently dropped

**Net effect: correct output, no crashes, no corrupted memory.** Same as if you'd pre-padded the inputs (but without the padding cost).

## Summary

| Library | Style | Better when |
|---|---|---|
| **TK** | floor div, strict shape requirement | You control your shapes (research code) |
| **Colleague's / our fix** | ceil div, OOB-tolerant | You want a general-purpose BLAS callable from any code |

Your colleague picked the **production-friendly** approach. TK picked the **research-purity** approach. **Both work; ceil-div just covers more shapes for free.**

## So our final fix package:

| Change | Source | Why |
|---|---|---|
| `make_smem_desc(addr, lbo, sbo)` parameterized | derived from TK + DeepGEMM | needed LBO + correct SBO for TN A |
| `a_off`, `b_off` use `* SWIZZLE_W` (not `* BM` / `* BN`) | derived from TK chunk_descriptor | smem is chunk-major after TMA+128B swizzle |
| `grid_m`, `grid_n` use ceil-div | colleague's choice | handle shapes not multiple of tile |

The first two are CORRECTNESS fixes (the real TN bug). The third is a USABILITY enhancement that unlocks the `tiny` shape. All three are now applied.

Want me to do anything else before you run on B300? Or shall I wait for the test log?

## About that last error — NOT a Makefile bug

```
CUDA error unspecified launch failure at examples/test_bgemm_sm103_bf16_tn_cluster.cu:145
make: *** [Makefile:197: run] Error 1
```

Here's the chain:

| Layer | What happened |
|---|---|
| The test program | Runs all **9 shapes** (tiny → square-4096), but crashes on a **later** shape (probably `llm-style 4096×4096×512` or `square-1024/2048/4096`) |
| `CHECK_CUDA` at [test file line 145](BLUBLAS_kota/BluBridge-BLAS/examples/test_bgemm_sm103_bf16_tn_cluster.cu#L145) | Detected the failure, printed the error, called `exit(1)` |
| Test program exit code | Non-zero (1) due to the abort |
| `make run` (`Makefile:197`) | Saw the non-zero exit code from the test → reported `Error 1` as it always does for any test failure |

So **the Makefile is fine** — it's just propagating the test program's exit code. The actual issue is **one of the bigger shapes after `xlarge` triggered a CUDA runtime error** (the unspecified launch failure). The "Error 1" from make is a downstream consequence, not the root cause.

## Why output looks like that order

Looking at the file:
```
line 14: CUDA error... (goes to stderr)
line 15: Device info... (goes to stdout, normal program output)
line 17-25: Test results table (stdout)
```

stderr and stdout interleave weirdly when redirected together. The `CUDA error` line was actually printed **last** chronologically (when the crash happened on shape 6+), but appears first in the file due to buffering differences.

## What's likely happening on the bigger shapes

The 6th shape in your test file is `llm-style 4096×4096×512`. For K=512 (much larger than BK=64), the kernel does `k_iters = K/BK = 8` MMA loops. There could be:
1. **Resource exhaustion** — bigger shapes use more grid CTAs, might exceed some cluster scheduling limit
2. **A shape-specific bug** — something only triggers when M or K is large
3. **Memory layout issue** — maybe square shapes hit a different code path

Your **colleague's output** (which you pasted earlier) successfully ran all 9 shapes. So the kernel CAN handle these — the question is whether your colleague's setup differs from yours somewhere besides the 3 changes we already applied.

`CUDA_LAUNCH_BLOCKING=1` makes CUDA report errors synchronously at the exact failing kernel launch — much clearer signal.

# Great question — and you're right to ask. These are two different things.

## "Where it stops" vs "why it stops"

| Question | What we learn | How to find it |
|---|---|---|
| **WHERE it crashes** | Which specific shape triggers the bug | Add `printf` before each shape, see the last one printed before crash. **5 minutes.** |
| **WHY it crashes** | What's actually wrong in the kernel | Isolate variables (K size, M/N size, grid count) and test combinations. **Longer, depends on findings.** |

**Both are needed**, but in this order: first WHERE (cheap, narrows scope), then WHY (uses the narrow scope to investigate efficiently). Without WHERE, the WHY investigation has too many variables.

## Why adding `printf` is the right next move

You can already infer **WHERE** logically (it stops after xlarge, so the 6th shape `llm-style` crashes — strong educated guess). But a `printf` removes the guess. It tells us with certainty:
- Is it really llm-style?
- Or maybe some other shape that we don't expect?

And the `printf` ALSO sets us up for the **WHY** investigation by letting us:
- Quickly bisect: add/remove shapes, re-run, see which exact shapes trigger it
- Compare attributes: is it K size? grid size? batch count? square vs rectangular?

So a `printf` isn't ONLY about "where it stops" — it's the diagnostic instrument that lets us isolate the variable for the WHY step.

### Step 3 — fix the root cause

Once we know what's special about the crashing shapes, we can look at the kernel for shape-specific bugs (K-loop bound check, TMEM access pattern, cluster sync issue, etc.).

# 🎯 NAILED IT — the bug is **K=512 + grid ≥ ~256 CTAs** combination

## Test results breakdown

| Shape | M | N | K | k_iters | CTAs | Wave count | Result |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| xlarge | 2048 | 2048 | 256 | 4 | 256 | ~2 | ✅ PASS |
| iso-K512-grid32 | 1024 | 1024 | **512** | **8** | 64 | 1 | ✅ PASS |
| **iso-K512-grid128** | **2048** | **2048** | **512** | **8** | **256** | **~2** | **❌ CRASH** |

## What this isolation proves

**Compare xlarge vs iso-K512-grid128**:
- Same M=2048, same N=2048, same grid (256 CTAs)
- ONLY K differs: 256 (xlarge) vs 512 (iso-K512-grid128)
- xlarge PASSES, iso-K512-grid128 CRASHES
- → **K=512 by itself is wrong with K=256-grid**

**Compare iso-K512-grid32 vs iso-K512-grid128**:
- Same K=512 (both have k_iters = 8)
- Same tile (128x128x64), same QUEUE_SIZE
- ONLY M/N (= grid) differs: 64 CTAs vs 256 CTAs
- Smaller grid PASSES, larger grid CRASHES
- → **K=512 alone is fine**

## The smoking gun: **`k_iters == QUEUE_SIZE` exactly AND multi-wave CTA scheduling**

QUEUE_SIZE is **8** in this config (computed in the launcher from smem budget).

- For K=64, 128, 256 → k_iters = 1, 2, 4 → **< QUEUE_SIZE** → no pipeline wrap → all pass
- For K=512 → k_iters = **8 = QUEUE_SIZE exactly** → exactly one full wrap of the producer queue per K-loop

When k_iters exactly equals QUEUE_SIZE:
- Producer cycles stage 0..7 → back to 0 in one K-loop
- `phase ^= (stage == 0)` fires exactly once per K-loop (at the wrap)
- After tile 1: producer's `phase = 1`, `stage = 0`
- After tile 2: producer's `phase = 0`, `stage = 0`
- Etc.

For shapes where grid ≤ ~144 CTAs (one wave), each CTA does only 1-2 tiles. The phase tracking happens to align with how empty_mbars get reused.

For shapes where grid > 144 CTAs (multi-wave), each CTA does many tiles via CLC's persistent dispatch. Sometime around the 3rd or 4th tile **on a CTA's persistent loop**, the producer's phase tracking diverges from the consumer's, OR an mbarrier expected-arrival count gets out of sync. The mbarrier_wait either times out or reads stale data → "unspecified launch failure".

## What's likely actually wrong in the kernel

Looking at the producer warp ([Bgemm_sm103_bf16_TN_cluster.cu:478-481](BLUBLAS_kota/BluBridge-BLAS/src/level3/batched_gemm/BF16/SM103/Bgemm_sm103_bf16_TN_cluster.cu#L478-L481)):

```cpp
int stage = 0, phase = 0, issued = 0;
// ...
for (int k = 0; k < k_iters; ++k) {
    if (issued >= QUEUE_SIZE)
        mbarrier_wait(empty_mbar_addr + stage*8, phase ^ 1);
    ++issued;
    // TMA load to stage
    stage = (stage + 1) % QUEUE_SIZE;
    phase ^= (stage == 0);
}
// After CLC fetch of next tile:
issued = 0;     // ← reset to 0, allowing first QUEUE_SIZE iters to skip wait
// but stage and phase carry over!
```

The `issued = 0` resets the wait check, but `stage` and `phase` carry over between tiles. After the FIRST tile with k_iters=8, `stage=0, phase=1`. For the SECOND tile, producer starts at stage=0 with phase=1. It issues 8 TMAs without waiting (because issued reset to 0). But each of those stages were JUST USED in the first tile and the consumer needs to commit empty_mbar before they're safe to reuse.

For k_iters < QUEUE_SIZE: producer naturally writes to unused stages first (e.g., for k_iters=4, stages 0..3 in tile 1, then stages 4..7 in tile 2 — no collision).

For **k_iters = QUEUE_SIZE**: producer writes to stages 0..7 in tile 1, then **immediately wraps and overwrites stages 0..7 in tile 2 before consumer might have caught up** — race condition.

For small grids, the consumer keeps up (few clusters, no backpressure). For large grids, the GPU is busy juggling many clusters and the consumer might lag, hitting the race.

## Three possible fixes

| Fix | Approach | Risk |
|---|---|---|
| **A** — Make QUEUE_SIZE smaller (e.g., force =4) so k_iters > QUEUE_SIZE always | Forces producer waits to fire on every wrap | Smaller smem queue = some perf loss |
| **B** — Don't reset `issued = 0` between tiles; let it monotonically grow | Producer correctly waits for the second-tile's first issue | Simple, low-risk |
| **C** — Make consumer commit `empty_mbar` BEFORE starting tile-N+1's MMA (force flush) | Strict per-tile barrier | More invasive |

## My recommendation

**Fix B** — remove the `issued = 0` reset. The check should be "have we issued ≥ QUEUE_SIZE total since kernel start?" not "have we issued ≥ QUEUE_SIZE in this tile?"

Want me to apply Fix B + confirm with another B300 run? It's a 1-line change and should make iso-K512-grid128 (and llm-style, square-*) all pass.

> tool `Bash`: Find the issued counter usage in our TN file

# Found it. **Line 492 — `issued = 0;` — is the bug.** Our code has it, colleague's doesn't.

## The exact bug

Line 492 of our TN_cluster.cu resets `issued = 0` between tiles. The colleague's code does NOT have this reset — `issued` grows monotonically across all tiles.

**Why this matters for K=512 + big grid**:

When `k_iters = QUEUE_SIZE = 8`, producer cycles through all 8 stages in one tile. Without `issued = 0`, the wait check `if (issued >= QUEUE_SIZE)` correctly fires on tile 2's first issue, forcing producer to wait for consumer's empty_mbar arrival before reusing stage 0.

With `issued = 0`, producer skips waits in tile 2's first 8 issues, **overwriting stages 0..7 before consumer has finished reading them** — race condition. Small grids hide it (consumer keeps up), large grids expose it.

## Applying the fix — delete line 492
> tool `Read`: /home/blu-bridge016/Downloads/Neural_Networks_exp_1926/BLUBLAS_kota/BluBridge-BLAS/src/level3/batched_gemm/BF16/SM103/Bgemm_sm103_bf16_TN_cluster.cu
> tool `TodoWrite`: 
> tool `Edit`: /home/blu-bridge016/Downloads/Neural_Networks_exp_1926/BLUBLAS_kota/BluBridge-BLAS/src/level3/batched_gemm/BF16/SM103/Bgemm_sm103_bf16_TN_cluster.cu
> tool `Bash`: Verify issued=0 reset removed + local build clean

# `issued = 0` removed + build clean ✓ — now the side-by-side comparison

## Differences I found between our file and colleague's

I just did a full line-by-line diff. There are **5 differences total**, only ONE is a functional bug:

| # | Diff | Ours | Colleague | Impact |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **1** | **`issued = 0;` reset between tiles** | Has it (line 492) | DOES NOT have it | **🔥 THE BUG — just fixed** |
| 2 | `make_smem_desc` parameterization | Runtime args `(addr, lbo, sbo)` | Template params `<RowStride, LeadingStride>` | Cosmetic (same values produced) |
| 3 | B descriptor LBO value | Always `BK * SWIZZLE_W * sizeof(bf16)` | Conditional: `0` when `BN/CTA ≤ 64`, else `BK*64` | Not a bug — when m=1 (our case BN/2=64), LBO is unused by hardware. Both values work. |
| 4 | Header name | `"mycublas.h"` | `"blublas.h"` | Library naming only — symbols match (`mycublasBgemmSM103_...` vs `blublasBgemmSM103_...`) |
| 5 | `256x256x64` entry point | Conditional fallback (`M % 512 == 0` → try 256 tile) | Always falls back to 128x128x64 | Colleague treats BM=256 as invalid; we partially try it |
| 6 | `s_arch_ok` device check | Present (we check `prop.major==10 && prop.minor>=3`) | Removed | Cosmetic guard, doesn't affect kernel behavior |

## Why diff #1 is the killer (and others aren't)

**`issued = 0;` reset** breaks pipelining when `k_iters == QUEUE_SIZE` (=8 for K=512 in our config) AND multiple tiles run per CTA (large grid + persistent CLC). After tile 1:
- All 8 smem queue stages are in-flight (producer wrote, consumer reading)
- Tile 2 starts → with `issued = 0`, producer skips wait → overwrites stages BEFORE consumer finishes
- Race condition → CUDA "unspecified launch failure"

Without `issued = 0`:
- After tile 1, `issued = 8`
- Tile 2 first issue: `issued >= QUEUE_SIZE` → wait fires → producer waits for consumer's empty_mbar → safe reuse

**For small grids**: each CTA runs only 1 tile, no inter-tile race possible, so the bug is hidden. That's why small/medium/large/xlarge all PASS.
**For large grids**: each CTA runs multiple tiles via CLC → race becomes possible → llm-style and beyond crash.

## Diff #3 (B's LBO) — worth fixing for cleanliness, not for correctness

When `BN/CTA == 64 == SWIZZLE_W`, the B operand fits in a single 64-element chunk in the N direction. The `m` count = 1. Per PTX 9.3 spec, LBO is multiplied by `m_idx ∈ [0, m-1]` → `m_idx = 0` always → `LBO * 0 = 0` contribution.

So:
- Colleague's `LBO = 0` works ✓
- Our `LBO = 8192` works too ✓ (because hardware multiplies by 0)
- But colleague's is **more correct semantically** ("LBO not used when m=1")

Not the bug, but a nice-to-have cleanup. I'll leave it as-is for now since changing it would mean adding template parameters and we have a working fix.

## Diff #5 (256x256x64) — irrelevant for our crash

The crash happens on the 128x128x64 path. The 256x256x64 entry isn't called by our test for the failing shapes.

## Final state — what's in our file now

| Fix | Status |
|---|---|
| `make_smem_desc` 3-arg with LBO/SBO | ✓ |
| `a_off`, `b_off` use SWIZZLE_W (not BM/BN) | ✓ |
| `LBO = BK * SWIZZLE_W * 2 = 8192`, `SBO = 1024` | ✓ |
| Launcher uses ceil-div for grid_m/grid_n | ✓ |
| **Producer's `issued` no longer reset between tiles** | ✓ **just fixed** |
| Local build clean | ✓ |

## But there's a new issue on the 256x256x64 path

The `256x256x64` test crashes on `medium-256` with **"illegal instruction"** (different from before — "illegal instruction" means the kernel tried to run a SASS opcode that doesn't exist on the hardware).

Look at the colleague's `256x256x64` entry point comment from output.md (lines 772-780):
```cpp
extern "C" void blublasBgemmSM103_bf16_tn_cluster_256x256x64(...) {
    cudaStream_t stream = handle ? handle->stream : 0;
    // BM=256 is architecturally invalid for 2-CTA cooperative MMA. Fall back to 128x128x64.
    dispatch_bgemm_tn_cluster(A, B, C, M, N, K, batchCount, alpha, beta, stream);
}
```

**Colleague treats `BM=256` as architecturally invalid** for the 2-CTA cooperative MMA path. They always fall back to 128x128x64.

Our code tries `BM=256` when `M % 512 == 0`:
- For `small-256` (M=256): M%512 != 0 → falls back to 128 → PASSES
- For `medium-256` (M=512): M%512 == 0 → uses BM=256 → **illegal instruction**

`BM=256` exceeds what `tcgen05.mma` allows for 2-CTA cooperative kind::f16 (max M per instruction is constrained). The SASS that nvcc emits for `BM=256` references an invalid encoding → hardware rejects with "illegal instruction".

## Easy fix — match colleague: always fall back to 128x128x64

## Journey summary — from 0/9 to 16/16

| Bug | Fix | Source |
|---|---|---|
| 1. `make_smem_desc` SBO hardcoded wrong for TN A | Parameterized with LBO + SBO | derived from PTX 9.3 spec |
| 2. LBO=0, SBO=1024 wrong values | LBO = `BK*SWIZZLE_W*2 = 8192`, SBO = `8*SWIZZLE_W*2 = 1024` | TK + DeepGEMM canonical |
| 3. `a_off`, `b_off` used `*BM` / `*BN/CTA` instead of `*SWIZZLE_W` | Use `*SWIZZLE_W` consistently | TK chunk_descriptor formula |
| 4. Floor-div grid math → `grid=0` for shapes < tile | Ceil-div + TMA OOB-fill | colleague's launcher |
| 5. `issued = 0` reset between tiles → race for K=512+ + big grid | Removed the reset | diff vs colleague's code |
| 6. `256x256x64` entry used invalid BM=256 path | Always fall back to `128x128x64` | colleague's comment in code |

Six distinct fixes, all in `Bgemm_sm103_bf16_TN_cluster.cu`. None in any other file.

```bash
        examples/test_bgemm_sm103_bf16_tn_cluster.cu

git commit -m "fix(tn): complete TN BF16 cluster kernel — 16/16 shapes PASS w/ 0.00 MaxRelErr

Six corrections to the TN cluster kernel, derived from ThunderKittens +
DeepGEMM + CUTLASS reference implementations and PTX ISA 9.3 spec:

1. make_smem_desc parameterized with (LBO, SBO) — descriptor now correctly
   encodes both fields per PTX 9.3 §9.7.17.4.1 Table 45.

2. For MN-major 128B swizzle bf16: LBO = BK * SWIZZLE_W * sizeof(bf16)
   = 8192 for BK=64;  SBO = 8 * SWIZZLE_W * sizeof(bf16) = 1024.

3. a_off / b_off use SWIZZLE_W (=64) as K-row stride, not BM/(BN/CTA).
   TMA + 128B swizzle produces chunk-major smem; +1 K row in a chunk is
   the swizzle atom width (128 B), not the full BM*sizeof row stride.

4. Launcher uses ceil-div for grid_m / grid_n so shapes smaller than one
   tile still launch with grid>=1; TMA OOB-fill handles partial tiles.

5. Removed 'issued = 0' reset between persistent CLC tiles. With
   k_iters == QUEUE_SIZE (K=512 in this config) AND large grids (multi-
   wave CTA scheduling), resetting hid the empty_mbar wait and let
   producer overwrite stages still in-flight on consumer side.

6. 256x256x64 entry now always falls back to 128x128x64. BM=256 is
   architecturally invalid for 2-CTA cooperative tcgen05.mma.kind::f16
   (per-MMA M caps at 256 = CTA_GROUP * BM; BM=256 produces illegal SASS).

Tested on B300 (sm_103a):
  - 128x128x64: 9/9 shapes PASS, MaxRelErr = 0.00e+00 (bitwise vs cuBLAS)
  - 256x256x64: 7/7 shapes PASS, MaxRelErr = 0.00e+00
  - square-4096: 1647 TFLOPS = 97% of cuBLAS"

git push origin main
```

## What we learned (for future debugging notes)

- **Reference implementations beat guessing every time.** TK + DeepGEMM canonical values cracked the descriptor bug. Don't try to derive PTX-spec stuff from scratch when working implementations exist.
- **`printf` instrumentation is essential** for narrowing crash sites. Saved hours of speculation about "which shape crashes".
- **Isolation tests** (the iso-K* shapes we added) made the "K=512 + grid" interaction obvious — single-variable bisection > multi-variable speculation.

🎉 Massive win. Push it and celebrate.

### CLAUDE [2026-06-30 19:45 IST]

# Great observation — yes, we're using the **same 128x128x64 kernel** for both API entry points. It's correct but not optimal.

## What's actually happening

Current state after the fixes:

| API call | Internal behavior | Actual tile used |
|---|---|---|
| `mycublasBgemmSM103_bf16_tn_cluster_128x128x64(...)` | → `launch_bgemm_tn_cluster<128, 128, 64>` | BM=128, BN=128 |
| `mycublasBgemmSM103_bf16_tn_cluster_256x256x64(...)` | → `dispatch_bgemm_tn_cluster` → same 128x128x64 launcher | BM=128, BN=128 (NOT 256) |

So the "256x256x64" name is misleading. **It's running the 128x128x64 kernel underneath.**

## Proof from your B300 numbers — identical throughput

| Shape | "128" call | "256" call | Delta |
|---|---:|---:|---:|
| small | 3.29 T | 3.31 T | ~0 (identical) |
| medium | 12.85 T | 12.87 T | ~0 |
| large | 50.46 T | 50.41 T | ~0 |
| xlarge | 303.94 T | 304.35 T | ~0 |
| llm-style | 906.88 T | 911.81 T | ~0 |
| square-4096 | 1646.84 T | 1648.64 T | ~0 |

**No throughput difference at all.** Both call sites run the same kernel binary.

## Why we can't just "use BM=256" directly

For `tcgen05.mma.cta_group::2.kind::f16`:
- The instruction's M dimension caps at `CTA_GROUP_SIZE * BM`
- With 2-CTA cooperative MMA: max `M = 2 * BM`
- The instruction's M field is 5 bits (encoded as `M >> 4`), max raw M = 256
- So `2 * BM = 256` → BM = 128 maximum

**BM=256 + 2-CTA cluster requires M=512 per MMA, which the instruction simply cannot encode.** That's why we saw the "illegal instruction" error before the fallback.

## What would actually be more efficient

Three ways to genuinely get bigger tiles:

### Option 1 — bigger BN (cheapest)
`128x256x64` with 2-CTA cluster: still BM=128 (instruction-legal), but each CTA handles `BN/2 = 128` N-elements. Total tile per cluster: M=256, N=256. **This is a real "256x256" tile per cluster.**

Cost: needs more smem (B tile doubles), so QUEUE_SIZE drops. Need to recompute the budget.

### Option 2 — single-CTA cta_group::1 with BM=256 (medium)
A separate `cta_group::1` kernel variant where one CTA handles the full M=256 tile alone. Loses the 2-CTA cooperation but allows real BM=256. Different kernel entirely.

### Option 3 — macro-tile via outer loop (most invasive)
Keep BM=128 cluster, but each CTA does **2 sequential 128x128 tiles** before moving on. Effectively a 256x256 "macro-tile" composed of 4 hardware tiles. Better L2/smem reuse without changing the MMA path.

## My honest take: leave it as-is for now

Reasons:
1. **Correctness is locked** — 16/16 PASS with bitwise precision. Don't risk regression.
2. **We're already at 97% of cuBLAS** on `square-4096` — the biggest, most compute-bound shape. Room for improvement exists on medium shapes (62-80%), but the win-per-effort ratio is lower.
3. The "256x256x64" entry point is essentially **dead code** right now — nothing in your dispatcher routes shapes to it that the 128x128 path wouldn't already handle. So the current naming is misleading but not harmful.

## What I'd recommend long-term

1. **Now**: commit the working 16/16 fix and ship it.
2. **Next sprint** (separate task): implement **Option 1 (128x256x64)** as a real bigger-tile variant, and update the dispatcher heuristic to choose:
   - 128x256x64 for `M*N >= 2048*2048` AND `M%256==0 AND N%256==0`
   - 128x128x64 otherwise

That would likely close most of the gap to cuBLAS on the medium/xlarge shapes.

## If you want a quick cosmetic cleanup right now

Either:
- **Rename** `mycublasBgemmSM103_bf16_tn_cluster_256x256x64` → `mycublasBgemmSM103_bf16_tn_cluster_128x128x64_alias` (truth-in-naming), OR
- **Remove** the 256x256x64 entry entirely and update the test to only use the 128x128x64 entry

But honestly I'd leave it alone for now — keeping the entry point allows future work to plug in a real bigger-tile kernel without changing the public API.

What do you want to do?

# Yes, **Option 1 (128x256x64)** is what I'd pick — easiest win with real throughput potential

## Why Option 1 over the others

| Option | What it does | My take |
|---|---|---|
| **1 — 128x256x64** (instantiate kernel with bigger BN) | BM stays at 128 (instruction-legal), BN doubles. 2-CTA cluster still does M=256, N=256 cooperatively. | **✅ My pick.** Leverages existing kernel infrastructure — just a different template instantiation. Smallest blast radius, biggest theoretical AI improvement. |
| 2 — Single-CTA cta_group::1 with BM=256 | Loses the 2-CTA cooperation. Whole new kernel variant. | ❌ Too invasive, loses 2-CTA throughput benefits. |
| 3 — Macro-tile via outer loop (4 sub-tiles per CTA) | Same hardware tile, bigger work-per-CTA. Better L2 reuse. | ❌ Most complex; reshuffles a lot of CLC + smem logic. |

## Theoretical gain of Option 1

Arithmetic intensity per MMA call:
- **128x128**: `2*128*128 / (128 + 128) = 128 FLOPs/byte` (B + A loads per K-step)
- **128x256**: `2*128*256 / (128 + 256) = 170 FLOPs/byte`

That's **~33% more FLOPs per byte loaded** — directly translates to better throughput on memory-bound shapes (medium/large), and same on compute-bound (where we're already at 97% of cuBLAS).

## smem budget check — does it fit?

For `<BM=128, BN=256, BK=64>` with `smem_budget = 227 KB`:

| Field | Old (128x128x64) | New (128x256x64) |
|---|---:|---:|
| `a_tile` | 64*128*2 = 16,384 B | 64*128*2 = **16,384 B** (same) |
| `b_tile` | 64*(128/2)*2 = 8,192 B | 64*(256/2)*2 = **16,384 B** (2×) |
| `tile_size` | 24,576 B | **32,768 B** |
| `store_tot` | 32,768 B | 32,768 B (same) |
| `QUEUE_SIZE` | 8 | **6** (drops from 8) |
| `smem_size` total | 229,376 B | **229,376 B** (still fits) |

QUEUE_SIZE drops 8 → 6, which is still > 2 (`static_assert` passes). Slight pipelining loss but extra AI more than makes up.

**Bonus**: QUEUE_SIZE=6 means `k_iters = QUEUE_SIZE` (=6) requires K=384, which isn't a common workload. The `issued = 0` race we just fixed becomes even less relevant.

## i_desc encoding check — is BN=256 legal?

For tcgen05.mma.kind::f16 (per PTX 9.3 Table 47):
- N field: bits 17-22 (6 bits), encoded as `N >> 3`
- Max raw N = 504 (= 63 << 3)
- For BN=256: `256 >> 3 = 32` (fits 6 bits) ✓

For M field: bits 24-28 (5 bits), encoded as `M >> 4`. For M=`2*BM=256`: `256 >> 4 = 16` (fits 5 bits) ✓ (same as current).

**Encoding is legal.**

## My plan — minimal change, dispatcher-driven

Modify the `_256x256x64` entry point so it uses **128x256x64** when shapes align, falls back to **128x128x64** otherwise:

```cpp
extern "C" void mycublasBgemmSM103_bf16_tn_cluster_256x256x64(
    mycublasHandle_t handle, int M, int N, int K, float alpha,
    const __nv_bfloat16 *A, const __nv_bfloat16 *B, float beta,
    __nv_bfloat16 *C, int batchCount) {
  cudaStream_t stream = handle ? handle->stream : 0;

  // 128x256x64: BM=128 (instruction-legal cap), BN=256 — 2-CTA cluster
  // gives M=256, N=256 per cluster.  Larger BN doubles arithmetic intensity
  // versus the 128x128x64 path at the cost of QUEUE_SIZE dropping 8 → 6.
  // Requires M % (CTA_GROUP_SIZE * 128) == 0 AND N % 256 == 0.
  if (M % (CTA_GROUP_SIZE * 128) == 0 && N % 256 == 0)
    launch_bgemm_tn_cluster<128, 256, 64>(A, B, C, M, N, K, batchCount,
                                          alpha, beta, stream);
  else
    dispatch_bgemm_tn_cluster(A, B, C, M, N, K, batchCount, alpha, beta,
                              stream);
}
```

# Mixed results — **128x256x64 isn't a uniform win.** Let me show you exactly where it helps and hurts.

## Apples-to-apples comparison (same M, N, K — different tile internally)

| Shape (M×N×K) | 128x128x64 | 128x256x64 | Delta | Verdict |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---|
| 256×256×128 | 3.28 T | 2.82 T | **−14%** | ⚠️ regression |
| 512×512×128 | 12.85 T | 11.11 T | **−14%** | ⚠️ regression |
| 1024×1024×128 | 50.41 T | 42.97 T | **−15%** | ⚠️ regression |
| 2048×2048×256 | 303.87 T | 305.18 T | +0.4% | ≈ same |
| 4096×4096×512 (llm-style) | 911.81 T | **1029.28 T** | **+13%** | ✅ real win |
| 2048×2048×2048 | 1110.96 T | 1118.60 T | +0.7% | ≈ same |
| 4096×4096×4096 | 1648.87 T | 1610.29 T | −2% | ≈ same |

## What this pattern tells us

**Small shapes regress (−14%)** because:
- 128x256 makes each cluster do 2× the work of 128x128
- Total number of tiles drops → fewer parallel CTAs → underutilization on B300's 144 SMs
- For M=256, N=256: 128x256 gives just **2 CTAs** total. 128x128 gives 4. Both bad, but 4 is less bad.

**Compute-bound shapes are flat** (square-2048, square-4096) because:
- Already saturating tensor cores
- Bigger AI doesn't help when ALU is already pegged

**llm-style wins (+13%)** because:
- K=512 is "medium" relative to M=N=4096
- This is exactly the regime where extra AI moves the needle — between memory-bound (small K) and fully compute-bound (huge K)
- This is the "LLM weight projection" workload shape, which is why it's named that

## My read — keep both paths, dispatch smartly

The +13% on llm-style is a real win. But blindly forcing 128x256 on all shapes loses 14% on small-medium ones. **The right answer is a heuristic dispatcher** that picks the tile per shape.

A simple heuristic that would have given us the BEST of both:

```cpp
static void dispatch_bgemm_tn_cluster_smart(
    const __nv_bfloat16 *A, const __nv_bfloat16 *B, __nv_bfloat16 *C,
    int M, int N, int K, int batchCount,
    float alpha, float beta, cudaStream_t stream)
{
  // 128x256x64 wins when both M and N are big enough to keep B300's 144 SMs
  // busy with the larger (= fewer) tiles. Empirically: M, N >= 4096 with
  // moderate K (≤ 1024) is the sweet spot — bigger AI helps and there's still
  // enough parallelism. For everything else, the 128x128x64 path wins.
  const bool prefer_big_tile =
      (M >= 4096) && (N >= 4096) &&
      (M % 256 == 0) && (N % 256 == 0) &&
      (K <= 1024);

  if (prefer_big_tile)
    launch_bgemm_tn_cluster<128, 256, 64>(A, B, C, M, N, K, batchCount,
                                          alpha, beta, stream);
  else
    launch_bgemm_tn_cluster<128, 128, 64>(A, B, C, M, N, K, batchCount,
                                          alpha, beta, stream);
}
```

This would:
- Use 128x256 ONLY for shapes like llm-style → keep the +13% win
- Use 128x128 for everything else → avoid the -14% regression on small shapes

Want me to add this heuristic to `dispatch_bgemm_tn_cluster` and route both API entries (`128x128x64` and `256x256x64`) through it?

## OR — different direction: improve the 128x128x64 path itself

Since 128x128x64 is winning on so many shapes, maybe we get more uplift by **tuning IT** rather than adding 128x256. Things that could move the needle:
- **Bigger QUEUE_SIZE** if we can shave smem elsewhere — better load/compute overlap
- **Hilbert curve tile ordering** for better L2 reuse (kernel mentions it but I should verify it's actually doing it)
- **Wave specialization** — separating producer/consumer warps more aggressively
- **Multi-stage epilogue overlap** with MMA

These are deeper changes but potentially uniform gains across all shapes.

## What I'd recommend

| Option | Effort | Expected gain |
|---|---|---|
| **A — add the heuristic dispatcher above** | 5 min | +13% on llm-style only (kept on a few shapes) |
| **B — tune 128x128x64 itself (Hilbert ordering, QUEUE_SIZE etc.)** | 1-2 hours | 5-15% potentially across MANY shapes |
| **C — combine A + B**: dispatcher + tune | 2-3 hours | Best total result |

What's your preference? My vote is **A first** (quick win, low risk), then **B** as the next focus area.

# YES — this is the RIGHT approach. Let's stop guessing and learn from cuBLAS directly.

Reverse-engineering what cuBLAS picks per-shape is the proper way to build a real dispatcher heuristic. Three ways to get this info, ordered from easiest to most thorough:

## Method 1 — Kernel-name profile via nsys (easiest, 5 min)

```bash
# on the B300 box
cd ~/gautam_k/blublas

# profile the test and dump every kernel that ran with name + duration
nsys profile --stats=true --force-overwrite=true -o /tmp/cublas_trace \
    ./build/test_bgemm_sm103_bf16_tn_cluster 2>&1 | tee /tmp/nsys.log

# show all kernel names called, sorted by frequency
grep -E "kernel|gemm|Time" /tmp/nsys.log | head -100
```

nsys's report will show every `cudaLaunchKernel` call. cuBLAS kernel names usually look like:
- `blackwell_bf16_gemm_128x128x64_tn_*`
- `cutlass3x_sm100_tnxx_*`
- `ampere_sgemm_64x64_tn`

So even just the names tell us the **tile size cuBLAS picked for each shape**.

## Method 2 — cuBLASLt heuristic logging (more detail, 10 min)

cuBLAS exposes its kernel-selection heuristic via env vars:

```bash
# captures cuBLAS's per-call decision, including the algo ID it picked
CUBLAS_LOGINFO_DBG=1 \
CUBLAS_LOGDEST_DBG=stdout \
CUBLAS_LOG_LEVEL=5 \
    ./build/test_bgemm_sm103_bf16_tn_cluster 2>&1 | tee /tmp/cublas_heur.log

# look for lines like "Selected kernel: gemm_..." or "Algo selected: ..."
grep -iE "select|algo|kernel|tile" /tmp/cublas_heur.log | head -50
```

This dumps cuBLAS's internal reasoning per shape. We see EXACTLY which kernel + tile config it picked.

## Method 3 — Static cuBLAS library inspection (deepest, 20 min)

Dump kernel names from `libcublas.so`/`libcublasLt.so`:

```bash
# locate cuBLAS libs
ldconfig -p | grep -iE "cublas|cublasLt"
# typically /usr/local/cuda/lib64/libcublasLt.so.13 or similar

# dump all device kernel symbols
cuobjdump --dump-elf-symbols /usr/local/cuda/lib64/libcublasLt.so.13 \
  | grep -iE "gemm.*sm10|gemm.*blackwell|sm_103|sm_100.*gemm|tn.*128|tn.*256" \
  | head -50

# alternatively, list ALL device symbols and grep for tile patterns
cuobjdump --dump-elf-symbols /usr/local/cuda/lib64/libcublasLt.so.13 \
  | grep -iE "_[0-9]+x[0-9]+x[0-9]+" | sort -u | head -100
```

This shows the **full kernel catalog** cuBLAS has available — every tile config they've shipped. We can see what they consider valid for B300 and copy the ones they actually pick for our shapes.

## Concrete plan — combine all three

### Phase 1 (on B300, 10 min): catalog cuBLAS's per-shape choices

```bash
cd ~/gautam_k/blublas

# (a) heuristic log + nsys trace in one run
CUBLAS_LOGINFO_DBG=1 CUBLAS_LOGDEST_DBG=stdout CUBLAS_LOG_LEVEL=5 \
    nsys profile --stats=true --force-overwrite=true -o /tmp/cublas \
    ./build/test_bgemm_sm103_bf16_tn_cluster 2>&1 \
    | tee /tmp/full.log

# (b) kernel names dump from cuBLAS lib
ls /usr/local/cuda/lib64/libcublas* 2>/dev/null
cuobjdump --dump-elf-symbols /usr/local/cuda/lib64/libcublasLt.so.* 2>/dev/null \
    | grep -iE "blackwell|sm_10[03]|_[0-9]+x[0-9]+_" \
    | head -200 > /tmp/cublas_kernels.txt
```

### Phase 2 (back here, ~30 min analysis): build the mapping table

Paste `/tmp/full.log` and `/tmp/cublas_kernels.txt` back to me and I'll:
1. Extract the cuBLAS kernel name for each of our 16 test shapes
2. Decode the tile dims from each name
3. Build a table: `(M, N, K) → cuBLAS_tile_config`
4. Compare against what OUR kernel picks
5. Propose a heuristic that matches cuBLAS's tile choices for shapes where they outperform us

### Phase 3 (back here, varies): implement the heuristic + benchmark

Based on what cuBLAS does, build a corresponding tile-selection logic in `dispatch_bgemm_tn_cluster`. Verify on B300.

## Quick note before you run

`CUBLAS_LOGINFO_DBG` works for the legacy cuBLAS API but for **cuBLASLt** (which `cublasGemmStridedBatchedEx` uses underneath), the env var is sometimes `CUBLASLT_LOG_FILE=/tmp/lt.log` or via the `cublasLtLoggerSetCallback` API. If method 2 returns empty, switch to:

```bash
CUBLASLT_LOG_FILE=/tmp/cublasLt.log \
CUBLASLT_LOG_LEVEL=5 \
    ./build/test_bgemm_sm103_bf16_tn_cluster 2>&1 | tee /tmp/run.log
cat /tmp/cublasLt.log | head -100
```

## Why this is better than guessing

Building a heuristic from cuBLAS's actual choices:
- ✅ Validated against millions of NVIDIA-engineer-hours of tuning
- ✅ Tells us BOTH what tile to pick AND which shapes to prefer it for
- ✅ Gives us a clear performance target ("match cuBLAS on these shapes, beat them on others")
- ✅ Side-effect: reveals tile configs we don't even have yet — could be a roadmap

Go ahead and run Phase 1 commands on B300, paste both `/tmp/full.log` and `/tmp/cublas_kernels.txt` back. I'll do the analysis and we'll have a real heuristic, not guesses.

first thing instead of guessing values i downloaded the full PTX ISA documentation locally (versions 8.8 through 9.3) so i have ground truth to grep instead of webfetching every time, then went into section 9.7.17.4 table 45 which actually documents the 64-bit shared memory descriptor format with LBO (leading dim byte offset) and SBO (stride dim byte offset) bit fields properly,

to be doubly sure i wasnt guessing i went into 3 independent reference implementations — thunderkittens descriptor.cuh, deepGEMM mma/sm100.cuh, and CUTLASS mma_traits_sm100.hpp — all three agreed on the canonical formula for MN-major 128B swizzle bf16 which is LBO = BK*SWIZZLE_W*sizeof(bf16) = 8192 bytes for BK=64 and SBO = 8*SWIZZLE_W*sizeof(bf16) = 1024 bytes, but our make_smem_desc was hardcoded to LBO=0 and SBO=1024 which is wrong for TN A operand because it spans multiple chunks in the M direction so LBO must actually be set to navigate between them,

ended up applying 6 separate fixes to the TN cluster kernel after this deep verification — parameterized make_smem_desc to take both LBO and SBO as args, changed a_off and b_off to use SWIZZLE_W (=64) as the K-row stride instead of *BM and *(BN/CTA_GROUP_SIZE) because TMA plus 128B swizzle actually produces a chunk-major smem layout not raw row-major, changed the launcher grid math from floor-div to ceil-div so shapes smaller than one tile still launch (TMA out-of-bounds fill cleanly handles partial tiles), removed the issued=0 reset between persistent CLC tiles which was causing a producer-consumer race when k_iters equals QUEUE_SIZE and grid spans multiple cluster waves (this was the specific cause of the launch crash on big shapes like llm-style 4096x4096x512), and made the 256x256x64 API entry always fall back to 128x128x64 because BM=256 is architecturally invalid for 2-CTA cooperative tcgen05.mma.kind::f16 (per-MMA M caps at CTA_GROUP*BM = 256 so BM=256 needs M=512 per MMA which the instruction doesnt encode so you get illegal instruction at runtime),

after each fix ran on B300 incrementally and confirmed shapes passing one by one — final result is 16 out of 16 shapes pass with maxRelErr exactly 0.00e+00 which is a bitwise match against cuBLAS, went from 0/9 to 16/16, throughput is also competitive — square-4096 (4096x4096x4096) hit 1646 TFLOPS vs cuBLAS 1685 TFLOPS which is 97% of cuBLAS on the biggest shape,
